Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm in the right place. I guess what made me come in is... well, I've been having a little trouble with my eating habits lately. Nothing too crazy, I don't think, but...

Um, well... I guess I kind of have a hard time stopping. It's not like I'm completely out of control or anything scary like that, but... 

It's more like, once I start, it feels like I’m almost watching myself just keep reaching for the next thing. Even while I’m sitting there thinking, *Jaime, stop, you have a budget report for work to finish and you’re not even enjoying this anymore*. I just kind of keep going until it's gone.

Um, well... I wouldn't say it's interfering *too* much. I mean, I'm still getting all my work done, and I haven't missed any of my classes. So I'm still handling everything I need to handle, you know?

Um, well... I mean, I guess it might be a little more than average? But I'm not, like, eating a whole grocery store or anything like that.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I guess I do get pretty full? But I mean, everybody overeats sometimes, right? Like at Thanksgiving or when something just tastes really good. 

But, um... I suppose by the end of it, my stomach does get kind of... tight? Like, under my work pants, it feels a bit uncomfortable. Sometimes I just have to sit there for a little bit because if I try to move around too much or bend over, I start to feel a little sick. 

But it's not like I'm doing it on purpose to hurt myself or anything! It just sort of sneaks up on me while I'm distracted with my laptop. I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is that normal for people who snack a lot?

Um, well... I guess it does happen pretty fast? I mean, I'm not really looking at the clock when I do it, so I'm not totally sure if it's exactly an hour or two.

But my hand just kind of keeps reaching for it anyway. It's almost like I'm just watching myself do it. \n\nI don't know if \"out of control\" is the right way to say it, because that sounds really bad, right? I just... I have a hard time stopping once the snacks are open. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot happen?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it happens somewhat often? I didn't really think it was a big deal, but... um... \n\nWhen I actually looked back through my journal recently, and, well, my food delivery charges... I realized it's been happening most weeks. Especially since all the restructuring started at work. It's usually, you know, more than once a week. Not just a random thing here and there like I thought. \n\nI mean, I know everyone stress-eats sometimes when work gets crazy, right? So maybe it's not that bad? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture. Is that... is that the kind of frequency you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't think so? I mean, I don't feel like I'm racing or anything like that. \n\nI guess I just kind of eat at a normal pace? Maybe a little steady, but not... not like I'm shoveling it in or anything embarrassing. I usually just sit there with whatever I ordered or opened, and I just keep taking bites. I don't think anyone watching would think I was eating unusually fast. \n\nIs that what you mean? Like, I'm not doing it in a weird way, right? I hope that answers what you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your daily life or causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say it's interfering *too* much. I mean, I'm still getting all my work done, and I haven't missed any of my classes. So I'm still handling everything I need to handle, you know?\n\nBut, um... I guess maybe it's affecting my social life a little bit? Like, my friends and family do these cooking nights and dinner get-togethers, and lately, I've just been... making excuses not to go. I just... I get kind of worried that if I'm around them and food is there, they might notice how much I eat, or... or they might figure out what I've been doing at home. \n\nSo I've been spending a lot more time alone when I'm not at work or school. Which isn't terrible, right? A lot of people like their alone time. But I guess it does bother me a little bit that I'm avoiding them. Does that count as what you're asking about? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's more than most people would eat under similar circumstances during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess it might be a little more than average? But I'm not, like, eating a whole grocery store or anything like that. \n\nIt's just... well, what actually made me decide to come in and talk to someone is that after work or class, I'll sit down with my laptop to unwind, and... I'll end up eating a whole takeout meal. And then maybe some chips, and some sweets, and... whatever snacks I have stashed in my desk drawer. \n\nI guess when I lay it all out like that, it is way more than I ever planned to eat. And, um, definitely more than I'd ever eat if anyone else was around. But a lot of people snack when they're studying or working late, right? So maybe it's not super abnormal? I just... I wanted to be honest with you about it. Is that the kind of amount you were thinking of?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat until you feel uncomfortably full during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I do get pretty full? But I mean, everybody overeats sometimes, right? Like at Thanksgiving or when something just tastes really good. \n\nBut, um... I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is that normal for people who snack a lot?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Um, well... Um, no, I don't think I really do that? I mean, I always make sure to get things that I actually want to eat. Like, I wouldn't just sit there and eat something I had zero interest in, you know? 

I always pick out my favorite snacks or order from places I really like. So I'm definitely interested in the food when I get it. Even if I kind of zone out while I'm working or watching a video like I mentioned earlier, it's still stuff I genuinely like having. I wouldn't just eat, I don't know, plain crackers or something I didn't care about at all just for the sake of eating. 

Is that... does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm not misunderstanding you. I just really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being helpful.

Like my hand is just moving on its own while I'm trying to unwind. \n\nI mean, I'm sure a lot of people zone out and eat a whole bag of chips without realizing it, right? It's just a bad habit I need to break. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of thing you were asking about... Um, yeah, I guess it kind of ties back to what I was saying a minute ago? I wouldn't say I have zero control, because I don't want you to think I'm completely, you know, unhinged or anything!

Which isn't terrible, right? A lot of people like their alone time. But I guess it does bother me a little bit that I'm avoiding them. Does that count as what you're asking about? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's more than most people would eat under similar circumstances during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess it might be a little more than average? But I'm not, like, eating a whole grocery store or anything like that. \n\nIt's just... well, what actually made me decide to come in and talk to someone is that after work or class, I'll sit down with my laptop to unwind, and... I'll end up eating a whole takeout meal. And then maybe some chips, and some sweets, and... whatever snacks I have stashed in my desk drawer. \n\nI guess when I lay it all out like that, it is way more than I ever planned to eat. And, um, definitely more than I'd ever eat if anyone else was around. But a lot of people snack when they're studying or working late, right? So maybe it's not super abnormal? I just... I wanted to be honest with you about it. Is that the kind of amount you were thinking of?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat until you feel uncomfortably full during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I do get pretty full? But I mean, everybody overeats sometimes, right? Like at Thanksgiving or when something just tastes really good. \n\nBut, um... I suppose by the end of it, my stomach does get kind of... tight? Like, under my work pants, it feels a bit uncomfortable. Sometimes I just have to sit there for a little bit because if I try to move around too much or bend over, I start to feel a little sick. \n\nBut it's not like I'm doing it on purpose to hurt myself or anything! It just sort of sneaks up on me while I'm distracted with my laptop. I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is that normal for people who snack a lot?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it does happen pretty fast? I mean, I'm not really looking at the clock when I do it, so I'm not totally sure if it's exactly an hour or two. \n\nBut, um, like I mentioned before about the takeout and the snacks... when I sit down with my laptop after class or work, it all just kind of happens in one sitting. Like, I'll finish the takeout, and then right after, I'll move on to the chips and the sweets from my drawer, all while I'm just sitting there trying to unwind. \n\nSo I guess it is all packed into that one chunk of time before I go to bed. I'm not just grazing all day, it's... it's mostly right then. But I feel like a lot of people probably eat their dinner and dessert together like that, right? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right... does that fit what you mean by a short period of time?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel self-critical or experience negative self-talk after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess I don't really beat myself up too much about it? I try to just move on, you know? \n\nLike, I might think, \"Oh, I probably shouldn't have eaten quite that much,\" but it's not like I'm sitting there calling myself names or crying about it or anything dramatic. I just sort of realize I overdid it and tell myself I'll do better tomorrow. \n\nI mean, everyone feels a little guilty when they eat too much junk food, right? It's just normal to want to be healthier. So I wouldn't say I'm super self-critical or anything... I try to stay positive. Is that... is that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the taste of the food when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, yeah, I think so? At first, anyway. Like, the takeout usually tastes really good when I start eating it. \n\nBut, um... honestly, after a while, I'm not really paying attention to how it tastes anymore. I'm just sort of eating while I look at my laptop, you know? It just becomes something I'm doing while I watch videos or whatever. \n\nI guess I'm not really savoring every bite like a food critic or anything, but it's not like I'm eating things I hate! I definitely pick out foods I like. That makes sense, right? Like, most people probably don't focus on the taste of every single chip they eat when they're watching TV. I hope that answers your question okay... Um, well... I don't think I'd say I'm eating *just* to eat? Because I really do start out wanting the food and enjoying it. It's not like I just go to the kitchen to mindlessly chew on something for absolutely no reason, you know? 

Like I mentioned, I always pick out my favorites. So the intention is definitely to enjoy a nice treat while I unwind or work. It's just that... after a while, the enjoyment sort of slips away or fades into the background, and I just keep going out of habit because I'm already doing it. But I definitely don't start out with the goal of just eating for the sake of eating.

I guess I do get pretty full? But I mean, everybody overeats sometimes, right? Like at Thanksgiving or when something just tastes really good. \n\nBut, um... I suppose by the end of it, my stomach does get kind of... tight? Like, under my work pants, it feels a bit uncomfortable. Sometimes I just have to sit there for a little bit because if I try to move around too much or bend over, I start to feel a little sick. \n\nBut it's not like I'm doing it on purpose to hurt myself or anything! It just sort of sneaks up on me while I'm distracted with my laptop. I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is that normal for people who snack a lot?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it does happen pretty fast? I mean, I'm not really looking at the clock when I do it, so I'm not totally sure if it's exactly an hour or two. \n\nBut, um, like I mentioned before about the takeout and the snacks... when I sit down with my laptop after class or work, it all just kind of happens in one sitting. Like, I'll finish the takeout, and then right after, I'll move on to the chips and the sweets from my drawer, all while I'm just sitting there trying to unwind. \n\nSo I guess it is all packed into that one chunk of time before I go to bed. I'm not just grazing all day, it's... it's mostly right then. But I feel like a lot of people probably eat their dinner and dessert together like that, right? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right... does that fit what you mean by a short period of time?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel self-critical or experience negative self-talk after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess I don't really beat myself up too much about it? I try to just move on, you know? \n\nLike, I might think, \"Oh, I probably shouldn't have eaten quite that much,\" but it's not like I'm sitting there calling myself names or crying about it or anything dramatic. I just sort of realize I overdid it and tell myself I'll do better tomorrow. \n\nI mean, everyone feels a little guilty when they eat too much junk food, right? It's just normal to want to be healthier. So I wouldn't say I'm super self-critical or anything... I try to stay positive. Is that... is that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the taste of the food when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, yeah, I think so? At first, anyway. Like, the takeout usually tastes really good when I start eating it. \n\nBut, um... honestly, after a while, I'm not really paying attention to how it tastes anymore. I'm just sort of eating while I look at my laptop, you know? I definitely pick out foods I like. That makes sense, right? Like, most people probably don't focus on the taste of every single chip they eat when they're watching TV. I hope that answers your question okay... I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you continue eating even when the food doesn't taste good to you anymore during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? Like, I mentioned that I stop really paying attention to the taste after a while. \n\nBut it's not like I'm forcing myself to eat garbage or anything! It's just... sometimes, once I get started, it's a little hard to stop? Like, I'll be sitting there, and I know I have this big budget report to finish for work, and I'll even be thinking to myself, \"Jaime, stop, you need to work, and this isn't even that good anymore.\" \n\nBut then I just sort of... keep reaching for the next thing anyway. It almost feels like I'm just watching myself do it, if that makes sense? Like my hand is just moving on its own while I'm trying to unwind. \n\nI mean, I'm sure a lot of people zone out and eat a whole bag of chips without realizing it, right? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, honestly, I don't think I've really noticed it decreasing? Like, the food doesn't ever start tasting bad or anything like that. I really don't want to make it sound like I'm just sitting there forcing down food I'm not enjoying, because that's not it at all. 

I think it's more just what I was trying to say a second ago—I just sort of zone out. But if I actually stop and focus on the food again, it still tastes really good. So the pleasure is definitely still there, I'm just maybe a little distracted by my spreadsheets or whatever I'm working on at the time. 

I hope that makes sense? I'm trying really hard to explain it right, so please let me know if I'm totally missing the point of your question! I just want to make sure I'm giving you good answers.

I'm not just grazing all day, it's... it's mostly right then. But I feel like a lot of people probably eat their dinner and dessert together like that, right? I mean, I guess I don't really beat myself up too much about it? I'm not just grazing all day, it's... it's mostly right then. But I feel like a lot of people probably eat their dinner and dessert together like that, right? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right... does that fit what you mean by a short period of time?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel self-critical or experience negative self-talk after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess I don't really beat myself up too much about it? I try to just move on, you know? \n\nLike, I might think, \"Oh, I probably shouldn't have eaten quite that much,\" but it's not like I'm sitting there calling myself names or crying about it or anything dramatic. I just sort of realize I overdid it and tell myself I'll do better tomorrow. \n\nI mean, everyone feels a little guilty when they eat too much junk food, right? It's just normal to want to be healthier. So I wouldn't say I'm super self-critical or anything... I try to stay positive. Is that... is that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the taste of the food when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, yeah, I think so? At first, anyway. Like, the takeout usually tastes really good when I start eating it. \n\nBut, um... honestly, after a while, I'm not really paying attention to how it tastes anymore. I'm just sort of eating while I look at my laptop, you know? It just becomes something I'm doing while I watch videos or whatever. \n\nI guess I'm not really savoring every bite like a food critic or anything, but it's not like I'm eating things I hate! I definitely pick out foods I like. That makes sense, right? Like, most people probably don't focus on the taste of every single chip they eat when they're watching TV. I hope that answers your question okay... I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you continue eating even when the food doesn't taste good to you anymore during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? Like, I mentioned that I stop really paying attention to the taste after a while. \n\nBut it's not like I'm forcing myself to eat garbage or anything! It's just... sometimes, once I get started, it's a little hard to stop? Like, I'll be sitting there, and I know I have this big budget report to finish for work, and I'll even be thinking to myself, \"Jaime, stop, you need to work, and this isn't even that good anymore.\" \n\nBut then I just sort of... keep reaching for the next thing anyway. It almost feels like I'm just watching myself do it, if that makes sense? Like my hand is just moving on its own while I'm trying to unwind. \n\nI mean, I'm sure a lot of people zone out and eat a whole bag of chips without realizing it, right? It's just a bad habit I need to break. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of thing you were asking about... I'm trying to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that there are times when you're not really interested in the food you're eating during these episodes, but you continue eating anyway?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I really do that? I hope I'm not misunderstanding you. I just really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess it kind of ties back to what I was saying a minute ago? It's like my brain knows I should stop, but my hands just keep moving. \n\nI mean, I'm sure everyone struggles with willpower when they're stressed out with work, right? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot happen?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it happens somewhat often? I didn't really think it was a big deal, but... um... \n\nWhen I actually looked back through my journal recently, and, well, my food delivery charges... I realized it's been happening most weeks. Especially since all the restructuring started at work. It's usually, you know, more than once a week. Not just a random thing here and there like I thought. \n\nI mean, I know everyone stress-eats sometimes when work gets crazy, right? So maybe it's not that bad? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture. Is that... is that the kind of frequency you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't think so? I mean, I don't feel like I'm racing or anything like that. \n\nI guess I just kind of eat at a normal pace? Maybe a little steady, but not... not like I'm shoveling it in or anything embarrassing. I usually just sit there with whatever I ordered or opened, and I just keep taking bites. I don't think anyone watching would think I was eating unusually fast. \n\nIs that what you mean? Like, I'm not doing it in a weird way, right? I hope that answers what you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your daily life or causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say it's interfering *too* much. I mean, I'm still getting all my work done, and I haven't missed any of my classes. So I'm still handling everything I need to handle, you know?\n\nBut, um... I guess maybe it's affecting my social life a little bit? Like, my friends and family do these cooking nights and dinner get-togethers, and lately, I've just been... making excuses not to go. I just... I get kind of worried that if I'm around them and food is there, they might notice how much I eat, or... or they might figure out what I've been doing at home. \n\nSo I've been spending a lot more time alone when I'm not at work or school. Which isn't terrible, right? A lot of people like their alone time. But I guess it does bother me a little bit that I'm avoiding them. Does that count as what you're asking about? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's more than most people would eat under similar circumstances during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess it might be a little more than average? But I'm not, like, eating a whole grocery store or anything like that. \n\nIt's just... well, what actually made me decide to come in and talk to someone is that after work or class, I'll sit down with my laptop to unwind, and... I'll end up eating a whole takeout meal. Like, I'll finish the takeout, and then right after, I'll move on to the chips and the sweets from my drawer, all while I'm just sitting there trying to unwind. \n\nSo I guess it is all packed into that one chunk of time before I go to bed. I'm not just grazing all day, it's... it's mostly right then. But I feel like a lot of people probably eat their dinner and dessert together like that, right? I mean, I guess I don't really beat myself up too much about it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those snacks, or do you feel like you can't stop until they're all gone?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I kind of have a hard time stopping. It's not like I'm completely out of control or anything scary like that, but... \n\nIt's more like, once I start, it feels like I’m almost watching myself just keep reaching for the next thing. Even while I’m sitting there thinking, *Jaime, stop, you have a budget report for work to finish and you’re not even enjoying this anymore*. I just kind of keep going until it's gone. \n\nIs that normal? I mean, I know a lot of people have trouble putting down a good bag of chips, right? I just hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I'd say I'm mostly in control? Like, I'm not doing anything dangerous. But, um... like I was just saying, it does sort of feel like I'm on autopilot sometimes. \n\nLike, I'll be sitting there, and my brain is going, *Jaime, you need to stop, you have that budget report for work to finish, and you don't even want another cookie*. But my hand just kind of keeps reaching for it anyway. It's almost like I'm just watching myself do it. \n\nI don't know if \"out of control\" is the right way to say it, because that sounds really bad, right? I just... I have a hard time stopping once the snacks are open. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot happen?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it happens somewhat often? I didn't really think it was a big deal, but... um... \n\nWhen I actually looked back through my journal recently, and, well, my food delivery charges... I realized it's been happening most weeks. Especially since all the restructuring started at work. It's usually, you know, more than once a week. Not just a random thing here and there like I thought. \n\nI mean, I know everyone stress-eats sometimes when work gets crazy, right? So maybe it's not that bad? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture. Is that... is that the kind of frequency you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't think so? I mean, I don't feel like I'm racing or anything like that. \n\nI guess I just kind of eat at a normal pace? Maybe a little steady, but not... not like I'm shoveling it in or anything embarrassing. I usually just sit there with whatever I ordered or opened, and I just keep taking bites. I don't think anyone watching would think I was eating unusually fast. \n\nIs that what you mean? Like, I'm not doing it in a weird way, right? I hope that answers what you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your daily life or causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say it's interfering *too* much. I mean, I'm still getting all my work done, and I haven't missed any of my classes. So I'm still handling everything I need to handle, you know?\n\nBut, um... I guess maybe it's affecting my social life a little bit? Like, my friends and family do these cooking nights and dinner get-togethers, and lately, I've just been... making excuses not to go. I just... I get kind of worried that if I'm around them and food is there, they might notice how much I eat, or... or they might figure out what I've been doing at home. \n\nSo I've been spending a lot more time alone when I'm not at work or school. Which isn't terrible, right? A lot of people like their alone time. But I guess it does bother me a little bit that I'm avoiding them. Does that count as what you're asking about? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food that's more than most people would eat under similar circumstances during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess it might be a little more than average? But I'm not, like, eating a whole grocery store or anything like that. \n\nIt's just... well, what actually made me decide to come in and talk to someone is that after work or class, I'll sit down with my laptop to unwind, and... I'll end up eating a whole takeout meal. And then maybe some chips, and some sweets, and... whatever snacks I have stashed in my desk drawer. \n\nI guess when I lay it all out like that, it is way more than I ever planned to eat. And, um, definitely more than I'd ever eat if anyone else was around. But a lot of people snack when they're studying or working late, right? So maybe it's not super abnormal? I just... I wanted to be honest with you about it. Is that the kind of amount you were thinking of?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat until you feel uncomfortably full during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I do get pretty full? But I mean, everybody overeats sometimes, right? Like at Thanksgiving or when something just tastes really good. \n\nBut, um... I suppose by the end of it, my stomach does get kind of... tight? Like, under my work pants, it feels a bit uncomfortable. Sometimes I just have to sit there for a little bit because if I try to move around too much or bend over, I start to feel a little sick. \n\nBut it's not like I'm doing it on purpose to hurt myself or anything! It just sort of sneaks up on me while I'm distracted with my laptop. I hope that doesn't sound too awful... is that normal for people who snack a lot?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it does happen pretty fast? I mean, I'm not really looking at the clock when I do it, so I'm not totally sure if it's exactly an hour or two. \n\nBut, um, like I mentioned before about the takeout and the snacks... when I sit down with my laptop after class or work, it all just kind of happens in one sitting. Like, I'll finish the takeout, and then right after, I'll move on to the chips and the sweets from my drawer, all while I'm just sitting there trying to unwind. \n\nSo I guess it is all packed into that one chunk of time before I go to bed. I'm not just grazing all day, it's... it's mostly right then. But I feel like a lot of people probably eat their dinner and dessert together like that, right? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right... does that fit what you mean by a short period of time?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel self-critical or experience negative self-talk after these episodes of eating a lot?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I guess I don't really beat myself up too much about it? I try to just move on, you know? \n\nLike, I might think, \"Oh, I probably shouldn't have eaten quite that much,\" but it's not like I'm sitting there calling myself names or crying about it or anything dramatic. I just sort of realize I overdid it and tell myself I'll do better tomorrow. \n\nI mean, everyone feels a little guilty when they eat too much junk food, right? It's just normal to want to be healthier. So I wouldn't say I'm super self-critical or anything... I try to stay positive. Is that... is that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you enjoy the taste of the food when you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, yeah, I think so? At first, anyway. I'm just sort of eating while I look at my laptop, you know? I hope that answers your question okay... I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you continue eating even when the food doesn't taste good to you anymore during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'm trying to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that there are times when you're not really interested in the food you're eating during these episodes, but you continue eating anyway?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I really do that? So I'm definitely interested in the food when I get it. Even if I kind of zone out while I'm working or watching a video like I mentioned earlier, it's still stuff I genuinely like having. I hope I'm not misunderstanding you. I just really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess it kind of ties back to what I was saying a minute ago? Like, the food doesn't ever start tasting bad or anything like that. I just want to make sure I'm giving you good answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel bad about yourself after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, well...
